# Prefix inventory: ethos, nexus, flow

Read-only inventory for flow 3ec648, made on 2026-10-02 at the main flow's request. It answers the living's order (typed, 2026-10-02): "vision has to become skills now. The skills have to be prefixed with "vision" and something else and maybe even split up", and "Let's focus on the stuff about ethos, what we're doing, the nexuses, and flow." The prefixes are not ruled yet. Nothing here proposes skill text.

Sections:
1. Skill lines classified as vision, knowledge, operation or unclassifiable.
2. Distilled `Vision/` statements, then raw records (`flows/*/vision/`, `flows/*/notion/`, `vision-raw/`), newest first by topic.
3. Tensions between skill lines and newer records.

## Method

- Skill sources: every Curriculum skill that names ethos, datom, protos, signal, sema, Nexus, a flow, a seat, a voice, a hook, the messenger or an aspect was read whole. The core set was classified line by line. For skills only partly about these topics (claude-harness, skill-designing), only the lines that touch them were classified.
- Classification is this subflow's judgment. "V" means a line states what the system should be and rests on a record of the living. A V line can also be witnessed as current fact; when it can, the note says so. Where a record was found, it is cited by path. Most V lines rest on distilled `Vision/<topic>.md` statements; their sources files list the raw records behind them.
- Raw records were extracted mechanically, with a script, from all 1,446 files under `flows/*/vision/` and `flows/*/notion/` and from `vision-raw/`.
  - Each `## ` heading starts one entry.
  - A record is filed under a topic in either of two cases. (a) Its file name is on the topic. (b) Its quoted words name the topic (marked †). For ethos, the word is "ethos". For nexus, the words are nexus, signal, datom, protos or sema. For flow, the words are subflow, seat, voice, hook, capsule, messenger, "main flow" or "flow nexus", or "flow" at least twice.
  - The quoted words are given verbatim. A short quote appears whole. A long one is cut to the sentences that name the topic, joined with "…", or to its first 350 characters.
  - The date comes from the record's provenance line or heading. Where neither has one, the date is the file's first git date or mtime, marked "(file date)".
- The same words relayed into several flows appear once per flow. For example, the 2026-10-02 flow-component and seat-name words appear in 91ea9f, 01e496, 42265e, 41fa34 and fe945a.
- Some files under `flows/48cff7/vision/` hold no words of the living. They are that flow's own proposals: skillKindsTaxonomy, skillSourceKinds and transcriptNexus. They are listed as found and carry no provenance line. By the vocabulary skill's term, they are vision impurities.

## 1. Skill lines classified

Authored home: `/git/github.com/LiGoldragon/Curriculum/skills/<name>.md` (Curriculum HEAD 927d056, the `Curriculum skills` variable in SKILL_VARIABLES.md). `/home/li/primary/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md` and the `.agents/`, `.codex/`, `.pi/` trees are generated from it. Line numbers below are Curriculum source line numbers; counts are of non-blank content lines (frontmatter, headings and code fences excluded; code lines counted).

Classes: V vision (the living's wish; cite), K knowledge (witnessable current fact), O operation (what to do on request), U unclassifiable. A line carrying two classes is filed under its dominant class; the other is named in the note.

## 2. Vision records, newest first

Format, distilled: Vision · topic file · date the heading line was last touched · «statement heading»: first sentences. Sources file references head each file.

Format, raw: `short flow id` file-topic [† = topic only mentioned; N = notion] · date · «entry heading»: “verbatim words” — provenance line.

### Distilled — Ethos


`Vision/ethos.md` — sources: 01a02a34 ethos; 01a02a34 schemaSyntax; f426777b spokenVocabulary; b675f3d9 kinds; 6863ef19 traitsAsCapabilities; 06196cc7 traitsAsCapabilities; 2b34fafa traitsAsCapabilities; 04db2fd2 kinds; 5abf3be8 encodedFormFingerprintTraitDesign; 4decf7 kinds; 2ef42163 ethos; e8c4cc61 kinds; b675f3d9 structuralParsing; ac1e9ec8 datomSyntax; e4a40e kinds; ad19b1 kinds; e8c4cc61 ethosFileAnatomy; e8c4cc61 kinds; 995a164e ethosTypes; 62022e8f ethosTypes; aa4c7747 interactions; aa4c7747 tuples; aa4c7747 ethosTraitSyntax; 2b34fafa ethosSourceFiles; 2b34fafa ethosNamespaces; b675f3d9 kinds; b675f3d9 structuralParsing; ad19b1 ethos; ad19b1 designPractice; 6329f1 ethos; e996e8 ethos; ad19b1 protos; 564f55 ethos; 564f55 datom; 564f55 signal; aa4c7747 ethos; e8c4cc61 ethosTypes; ba906ae2 signalIsOurMessagingLayer; 62022e8f designPractice; fe34eb ethos

- Vision · ethos · 2026-09-10 (heading last touched) · «Zero»: Ethos Zero was first named Ethos Monolith; the two are the same thing. Zero as in version 0: no daemon yet, no Nexus.
- Vision · ethos · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Roots»: Library, Signal, Sema. No version in a file.
- Vision · ethos · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Declaration: File»: The unit is File: one file, one Rust module. No namespace inside a file.
- Vision · ethos · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Imports»: An import names a source and a type: `protos:String` or `protos:[ String Integer ]`. An explicit import and an intrinsic name mean the same thing.
- Vision · ethos · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «What a declaration turns into»: A declaration turns into the Rust type with named fields, bearing the datom kinds through the derive, which Ethos Zero emits with the type. A field is named after its type in snake case; a constructed type type-first, `string_vector`, `lock_option`; a repeated type as first and second.
- Vision · ethos · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Inline types»: A type may be declared where it is used. The inline struct or enum is a full type whose derived name carries an underscore, non-idiomatic for a Rust type, so it never collides and reads at a glance as inferred from the sugar.
- Vision · ethos · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «A variant named as a defined type carries that type»: When a variant's name is a type already defined in the library, that type is the data the variant carries. Nothing further is written.
- Vision · ethos · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «A variant may declare its payload inline»: Instead of naming a defined type, a variant may declare what it carries in place: a vector, a struct, or an enum, each a full type with a derived name, recursively. ``` Library [] ; imports [ FilePath.String ; types GenerationFailure.[ SyntaxError.Vector<FilePath> ; the payload declared inline: a ve…
- Vision · ethos · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Every declared type bears both kinds»: Ethos Zero emits `Datomizable` and `Compositional` on every struct and enum it generates, so every ethos-declared type always bears both kinds and no declared type can exist without them. An alias bears them through the type it names: an alias is not a new type and cannot carry a derive.
- Vision · ethos · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «The datom kinds are compiled in only where text is spoken»: A generated signal library bears `Datomizable` and `Compositional` conditionally, under a feature the CLI and client enable and the Nexus does not. The Nexus compiles the same types without any textualization capability and without datom-codec as a dependency.
- Vision · ethos · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Shapes and placement»: Each section has its own parsing context; placement carries the meaning. Head then symbol is a variant of `Query` or `Response` in a Signal's first two sections, an alias in the types section.
- Vision · ethos · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Kinds are explicit; bodies are hand-written»: A kind is declared, never inferred; an association asserts the type bears it. The generated Rust carries a compile-time assertion that the type bears the kind; the interaction body is hand-written Rust.
- Vision · ethos · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Kind syntax»: A simple kind opens with a bracket after the dot. Its capabilities sit inside.
- Vision · ethos · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Associations»: An association declares that a type bears a kind: the type's name, a dot, a bracket of its kinds. In the Signal and Sema roots the associations of the query, response and record types are implied and never written.
- Vision · ethos · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Spacing»: Space the delimiters and the inner content. Ethos follows the canonical protos print: a space inside every bracket and brace at both ends when non-empty.
- Vision · ethos · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Generation»: By request to ethos-zero, which is not a daemon, hence its name; committed, held fresh by a test. ``` ethos-zero 'Generate.{ /abs/orchestrate.ethos /abs/out }' ```
- Vision · ethos · 2026-09-04 (heading last touched) · «Kind»: Kind is the word for the bearer of capabilities: something that can run is a runner, Runnable is its kind, and run is its capability, a function the kind has. Trait is set aside as acoustically ambiguous.
- Vision · ethos · 2026-09-04 (heading last touched) · «Naming»: Kinds are qualifier-named: Runnable, Textualizable, Structural, Embodied. Run is not a kind.
- Vision · ethos · 2026-09-04 (heading last touched) · «Identity»: A kind is identified as a Rust trait is, by its name and its constraints, written as one head: Processable<[Clonable Sendable] Serializable>. A constraint is a kind, or a bracket of kinds: what Rust writes as a generic parameter with its bounds, ethos writes as the bounds alone, since in ethos there…
- Vision · ethos · 2026-08-24 (heading last touched) · «What Ethos is»: Ethos is the schema language. Of the two main syntaxes most agents will face, Ethos specifies the types and Datom fills them with data.
- Vision · ethos · 2026-08-24 (heading last touched) · «Why Ethos»: All the legacy languages have a high noise ratio. Some lisps came close but lacked the correctness of Rust or Haskell; those have the correctness but allow no higher layer of abstraction that keeps the correctness of the whole.
- Vision · ethos · 2026-08-24 (heading last touched) · «Non-repetition»: Any repetition in ethos syntax is an implementation failure. Ethos aims to be the most terse, non-repetitive syntax ever made.
- Vision · ethos · 2026-08-24 (heading last touched) · «Self-description»: A datom object's basic CLI help emits the Ethos that describes its anatomy. The wanted mechanism extends this: point at any object — CLI now, Mentci later — and its Ethos prints, self-describing and self-evident.
- Vision · ethos · 2026-08-24 (heading last touched) · «Horizon»: Ethos will eventually replace everything, Rustlang becoming its assembly layer. Designs are chosen for that horizon; what it enables — generator emission among it — comes in its time.

`Vision/archive-ethosMonolith.md` — sources: (no sources file)

- Vision · archive-ethosMonolith · 2026-08-24 (heading last touched) · «Origin»: All our systems will be Nexuses, and the correct three-nexus ethos stack is the desired stack — but it is too complex to go for directly, and the previous effort devolved into agent hallucinations for lack of proper instructions. The monolith is the short-term path that brings ethos into production:…
- Vision · archive-ethosMonolith · 2026-08-24 (heading last touched) · «Name»: First named ethos-rust, the schema-rust analogue; then renamed ethos-monolith: it has no nomos and no logos component and goes straight to Rust — a monolith.
- Vision · archive-ethosMonolith · 2026-08-24 (heading last touched) · «Shape»: The monolith will itself be a Nexus. Nexus by itself names our specifically designed daemon — distinct from Nexus Core, the runtime engine — and executables are named component-nexus.
- Vision · archive-ethosMonolith · 2026-08-24 (heading last touched) · «Purpose»: An incremental implementation and bootstrap process, so that ethos and datom get written and read as soon as possible, without cutting corners, and components start being written in ethos.
- Vision · archive-ethosMonolith · 2026-08-24 (heading last touched) · «Vocabulary carried»: The Signal, Nexus, SEMA vocabulary and principles are kept; nothing is bound to how they were used and implemented in the past. Nexus is authored in ethos so its main operations are visible.
- Vision · archive-ethosMonolith · 2026-08-24 (heading last touched) · «Readiness»: Ethos serves new work in place of legacy schema once the monolith is ready to use; readiness is witnessed.


### Distilled — Nexus and its substrate


`Vision/nexus.md` — sources: e06e4c07 nexus; 01a03d6e nexus; acbb6006 nexus; 98fbfa47 metaCliIsComponentDashMeta; 012fbf07 threeStacks; 15b67974 actorLibrary; 564f55 nexus; 01a05487 nexus; db97561c nexus; fd301d9a nexusTraits; f426777b nexusTraits; f426777b ethosSourceFiles; b675f3d9 ethosMonolith; fe34eb nexus

- Vision · nexus · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «A kind of thing»: Nexus is our word for the style of component that speaks signal and uses a similar database.
- Vision · nexus · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Library and daemon»: Every component built from now on is a Nexus. The nexus repository is the library that defines the core of a Nexus component.
- Vision · nexus · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Universal traits first»: The basic ontology of an actor and dataflow system is designed before implementation; signal and sema are designed against it as if new, the old code at most inspiration.
- Vision · nexus · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Processing is for the effect»: An object enters a Nexus for the effect; the response follows as an effect of it. Conversion is the wrong frame for it.
- Vision · nexus · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Documents»: The nexus and sema documents are undesigned; when they are designed they live in the Nexus's main repository.
- Vision · nexus · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Why everything is a Nexus»: Everything built from now on is a Nexus, and what was built in another shape is rewritten as one. The consistency creates reliability, quality, and clarity.
- Vision · nexus · 2026-08-27 (heading last touched) · «A Nexus is the whole»: A Nexus is the whole long-running component: the process, its sockets, and the signal contracts it is compiled with. Nexus is its name; daemon is not.
- Vision · nexus · 2026-08-27 (heading last touched) · «Sockets»: A Nexus opens at least two sockets. The ordinary socket serves ordinary peers.
- Vision · nexus · 2026-08-27 (heading last touched) · «Default clients»: A client is a separate program from the Nexus. For now the default clients are packaged with the Nexus as separate crates of its repository, which is a multi-crate repository: one datom-converting CLI per socket, however many sockets the Nexus has, at least two.
- Vision · nexus · 2026-08-27 (heading last touched) · «Signal only»: Every client speaks to a Nexus in pure signal, fully binary. A Nexus speaks only the signal contracts it is compiled with; two of these are its own, one per socket.
- Vision · nexus · 2026-08-27 (heading last touched) · «The graph»: A Nexus is a vertex in the graph of nexuses. An edge joins two vertices and carries one contract.
- Vision · nexus · 2026-08-27 (heading last touched) · «Routing»: Signals cross the network through a router. The router tells signal types apart by an enum that wraps the objects, held in the signal repository, which every component depends on.
- Vision · nexus · 2026-08-27 (heading last touched) · «Configuration»: A Nexus starts with no arguments and there is no bootstrap binary. Its executable holds a default configuration as a constant.
- Vision · nexus · 2026-08-27 (heading last touched) · «First configuration»: A Nexus keeps a standard metadata tree. In it a type records whether the meta Configure was ever done; that record is reversed only on the meta socket, and while it is unset Configure is accessible on the ordinary socket.
- Vision · nexus · 2026-08-27 (heading last touched) · «Repositories»: A component has three repositories: its main repository, holding all its code, and two signal repositories — one for the ordinary socket's contract, one for the meta socket's. Shared kinds go into reusable libraries, which are encouraged.
- Vision · nexus · 2026-08-27 (heading last touched) · «Actors»: The engine inside a Nexus is driven by Kameo actors. The standards of their use are still to be designed.
- Vision · nexus · 2026-08-27 (heading last touched) · «Splitting a Nexus»: A Nexus deals with a domain. When its features grow too many, splitting one or more nexuses out of it is considered.
- Vision · nexus · 2026-08-27 (heading last touched) · «Observation by subscription»: State is observed by subscription: the subscriber receives the state on open, then each change as it happens.
- Vision · nexus · 2026-08-27 (heading last touched) · «Polling is forbidden»: Polling is forbidden; a correct system goes quiet when nothing changes.

`Vision/signal.md` — sources: 564f55 signal; 564f55 protos; 55d18f4f signalIsOurMessagingLayer; 6863ef19 signalIsOurMessagingLayer; ba906ae2 signalIsOurMessagingLayer; 98fbfa47 metaSignalNotOptional; fe34eb signal

- Vision · signal · 2026-09-10 (heading last touched) · «Protocol»: Signal is portable rkyv plus whatever protocol is standardized on top of it. The protocol is to be decided.
- Vision · signal · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Name»: The serialized form is called signal.
- Vision · signal · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «What signal is»: Signal is the messaging layer: fully binary, portable rkyv fixed across endianness, fully typed with both sides knowing the full schema, nothing on the wire labeling itself. It is one step from the composition, beside the text chain.
- Vision · signal · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Query and response»: A Signal declares queries and responses; input and output are too low-level for it. ``` Signal [] ; imports [ Lock.LockRequest Release.LockId ] ; queries [ Locked.Lock Released.Lock ] ; responses [ LockId.Integer ; types LockName.String LockRequest.{ LockName } Lock.{ LockId LockName } ] ```
- Vision · signal · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Text and signal»: The textual form is datom; a CLI actualizes it and sends signal; a Nexus never textualizes.
- Vision · signal · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Meta signal»: The meta signal is never optional: the daemon is configured only over its meta surface.

`Vision/sema.md` — sources: 564f55 sema; 564f55 ethos; f426777b ethosSourceFiles; 62022e8f designPractice; aa4c7747 ethosMonolith

- Vision · sema · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «What sema is»: Sema is the database engine of a Nexus, authored in Ethos so the stored types are visible; its root, Sema, declares record types. It matters more than nexus, because operational editing should yield the migration with the edit.

`Vision/datom.md` — sources: ac1e9ec8 datomSyntax; ac1e9ec8 datomIsData; 01a03eda datomInteger; 04db2fd2 datomMaps; 04db2fd2 datomNexus; 04db2fd2 text; 04db2fd2 textualTypes; 04db2fd2 anatomy; 04db2fd2 portion; 04db2fd2 directionAsymmetry; e8c4cc61 datomSyntax; e8c4cc61 datomizable; e8c4cc61 protos; 62022e8f datomSyntax; 62022e8f kinds; 62022e8f symbols; 62022e8f headedAndContained; 995a164e datomSyntax; 01a04339 datom; 01a035d3 rustCodeFromTheData; 01a03d6e dotosFiles; 01a03d6e ethosInterfaces; a5587095 structuredStringType; 5abf3be8 colonLegalInStringPosition; 06196cc7 datomSyntax; b675f3d9 structuralParsing; 4decf7 datomSyntax; e4a40e datom; e4a40e vocabulary; e4a40e newtypeWrappingAndSingleFieldStructs; 06196cc7 encodedFormIsTheCode; a5587095 datomSyntax; a5587095 threeStacks; a5587095 colonFormTransformerSyntax; a5587095 protosIsTheSharedStyle; 01a03eda datomSyntax; ad19b1 meaning; ad19b1 datom; ad19b1 protos; e996e8 datom; 564f55 datom; 564f55 protos; 4d5fc7da datom; 1a6ca4 datom; fe34eb datom; 542442 datom; f38926 meaningLanguage

- Vision · datom · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «A datom is a form at a path»: ```rust pub struct Datom { pub path: Path, pub form: Form } pub enum Form { Struct(Vec<Datom>), Vector(Vec<Datom>), Variant(Symbol, Box<Datom>), Bare(String), String(String), Meaning(Opaque) } ```
- Vision · datom · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Strings»: Two string forms. The bare form, whose name is bare: a run with no space and no delimiter glyph, which may be a whole sentence written without spaces in any casing; "word" does not do it justice.
- Vision · datom · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Syntax»: Structure is the word for every unit of the text: enclosed when it stands between its delimiters, unenclosed when bare. A headed structure is a head, a separator and a body; the dot is the separator, written right after the head, and it opens the body's delimiter.
- Vision · datom · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «The datom composes; the type states its positions»: The descent into a composition is the datom's act, written once. What only the type can supply, its positions in order, is stated by the type through the derive, so the reading of the tree, arity, budget, locus, lives in one place and no type repeats it.
- Vision · datom · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Any Rust type»: Datom is used on more than ethos-declared types. Any Rust type bears the two kinds through a derive in datom-codec, with no attributes, because datom is structural all the way down: field order is position order, a field's type is the position's type, a bare variant carries nothing, a single-field v…
- Vision · datom · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «From text and back»: A potential, text that may become a T, owns the budget and actualizes by the descent written once: protosize, datomize, compose. A composition becomes text by the open chain.
- Vision · datom · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Containers»: Vector, Option, Result, Box bear the kinds once, generically; Option and Result read as ordinary variants. ``` [ Some.42 None ] Ok.{ Ada 1990 } Err.«no such lock» ```
- Vision · datom · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Errors»: An error names the layer that raised it and the path of the datom where it arose; the extent is the protos node at that path. An error is itself datomizable.
- Vision · datom · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Omittable fields»: Not yet; a written datom gives every position. ``` Deploy.{ ouranos } ; Arity.{ 2 1 } ```
- Vision · datom · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Repository»: Everything moves to Datom: all of the stack, Horizon, Lojix, everything; no Dotos file remains. Datom's own line of descent is NOTA, which also passed through the temporary name Dotos; that old notation stays behind, frozen, and may be called legacy.
- Vision · datom · 2026-09-05 (heading last touched) · «Map»: A map is a container whose keys are known only from the data: the reader learns the shape by reading, where a struct's shape is known before reading. Datom is typed from the position down: a position knows its type before the text is read, and a container that reveals its shape only in the text cont…
- Vision · datom · 2026-09-03 (heading last touched) · «The interface shape»: A program's configuration surface is the datom's shape itself, as the ethos interface declares it: a data enum at the root whose variants are the main operations. A variant's data carries what follows: another enum where sub-operations are wanted, a struct or vector for final options, and a struct m…
- Vision · datom · 2026-08-24 (heading last touched) · «Name»: Datom is the psyche’s own coinage for the new data notation, the successor to NOTA and to the rejected name Dotos. The name was chosen for its energetic power and to echo what the notation is: data, strictly typed, super dense, no field names.
- Vision · datom · 2026-08-24 (heading last touched) · «Nature»: Datom is the most advanced textual data format in the world. It carries data, strictly typed, and its whole work is serialization and deserialization: carrying data between text and typed form.
- Vision · datom · 2026-08-24 (heading last touched) · «De/serialization»: Schema-driven and positional: the reader walks the expected type, writing is the exact reverse projection, and decoding lands directly in the typed Rust compositions. A datom on the way in is a potential datom, untrusted until it matches its type; on the way out it is a datom.
- Vision · datom · 2026-08-24 (heading last touched) · «Relation to Ethos»: Datom and Ethos are different languages that share an approach, not a parser. What they may share is a substrate, kinds with a shared implementation and types; the universal substrate machinery is homed in protos, all dialects ride it, and datom is the pure-data dialect on it.
- Vision · datom · 2026-08-24 (heading last touched) · «Meaning»: Meaning is the structured string: text that carries, besides its words, the emphasis and the other structural aspects a plain string simply lacks, an annotated string, meant to revolutionize the performance of thinking machines on text. The aim is the most advanced structured meaning system ever mad…

`Vision/protos.md` — sources: a5587095 protosIsTheSharedStyle; ba906ae2 protosIsTheSharedStyle; ba906ae2 encodedFormIsTheCode; e4a40e protos; 04db2fd2 anatomy; 04db2fd2 multiPass; 04db2fd2 portion; 04db2fd2 delimiters; e8c4cc61 protos; e8c4cc61 prospective; e8c4cc61 kinds; 62022e8f kinds; 62022e8f layers; 62022e8f concept; 62022e8f passes; 62022e8f vocabulary; 2ef42163 kinds; b675f3d9 structuralParsing; b675f3d9 kinds; 1c282d protosizable; 1c282d vocabulary; ad19b1 ethos; 6329f1 protos; 6329f1 vocabulary; 04db2fd2 directionAsymmetry; ad19b1 protos; e996e8 protos; 564f55 protos; 564f55 datom; 564f55 signal

- Vision · protos · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Layers»: Four layers, text above and the value below: the textual layer; the protosic layer, whose root type is the `Protos` enum; the conceptual layer, which is the datomic, ethosic, logosic, or nomosic layer according to the language; and the compositional layer, the composition, the value put together in …
- Vision · protos · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Every layer carries its own context»: A value at a layer contains the context it makes sense in, in both directions, and no layer carries a fact that belongs to another. The extent, where a structure sits in the text, is a fact of the protosic layer, and every `Protos` node carries one.
- Vision · protos · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Kinds are borne by the type converted and named for the layer it becomes»: No type bears a kind two layers away. A chain is written in the open where it is used, never folded into a kind on its first type.
- Vision · protos · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Signal is parallel»: Signal is not a layer of the chain: a parallel structure exchanging compositions in one step, an rkyv serialization carrying our own protocol.
- Vision · protos · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Delimiters»: Five pairs. `{ }`, `[ ]`, `< >` structural; `« »` opaque, every glyph content; `( )` reserved for meaning, its type unspecified, read by balance as opaque until it is.
- Vision · protos · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «String, escape, error»: The text type is `String`. A closing guillemet inside a string is escaped with a backslash, so the ascent never refuses.
- Vision · protos · 2026-09-04 (heading last touched) · «What Protos is»: Protos is the name for the style all the dialects share. The context-switching parse, the delimiters, the heads, the recursive structure — this is the code that can be shared between all parsers and belongs in protos.
- Vision · protos · 2026-09-04 (heading last touched) · «What Protos knows»: Protos is only about structure. It has nothing to do with struct and vector, and it only understands form: the syntactic structure.
- Vision · protos · 2026-09-04 (heading last touched) · «Structure»: Structure is the word for every unit of the text; its type is the `Protos` enum: headed, enclosed, opaque, or bare. A headed structure is a head, a separator and a body.
- Vision · protos · 2026-09-04 (heading last touched) · «Multi-pass»: Multiple passes are wanted over a single pass, because a single pass creates corner-cutting bad design. The multiple steps create a mental model of the machinery, which enforces a correctness in the code that is millions of times more beneficial than the cost of doing these multiple passes.
- Vision · protos · 2026-09-04 (heading last touched) · «Canonical print»: It is canonical, and it is considered good style, to leave a space between the delimiters and the content, except inside the guillemets, where every glyph is content and a space would be load-bearing. Space the delimiters and the inner content.

`Vision/orchestrate.md` — sources: 01a03d6e orchestrateDeployment; 01a03d6e orchestrateSkill

- Vision · orchestrate · 2026-08-27 (heading last touched) · «Deployment»: Orchestrate is deployed unconditionally, in the home, for every user. Its meta binary is part of it; a deployment without meta-orchestrate is wrong.
- Vision · orchestrate · 2026-08-27 (heading last touched) · «The skill»: The orchestrate skill covers ordinary operations only; meta operations are outside it.

`Vision/meaning.md` — sources: f38926 meaningLanguage; b81560 operational-asyncSubflowsAndMeaningLanguage; b81560 operational-meaningLanguageLogographic; b81560 operational-meaningLanguageAnnotationLayers; b81560 operational-meaningContentAddressedAnnotation; b81560 operational-meaningGarbageCollectionAndOntology; b81560 operational-vaisheshikaRuledAndSyntaxQuestion; b81560 operational-meaningDualSanskritEnglishNames; 5851f4 ashtadhyayiKnowledgeBase

- Vision · meaning · 2026-09-20 (heading last touched) · «What the meaning language is»: The meaning language is a computer language that is purely logographic, the way Hanzi characters are logographic: a sign stands for a meaning rather than for a sound. Its signs are typed structs and enums forming one ontology, defined in Ethos and backed by Rust, and held as a datom graph — a body o…
- Vision · meaning · 2026-09-20 (heading last touched) · «Roots»: The roots of the ontology are the seven Vaiśeṣika categories, and its verbs follow the Aṣṭādhyāyī of Pāṇini, which an earlier ruling makes the base for how the system thinks, communicates, and classifies things in the world (flow 5851f4). How the two divide beyond that is not yet ruled.
- Vision · meaning · 2026-09-20 (heading last touched) · «Verbs»: The verb set is Sanskrit. What the verbs define are the situations a statement can be in: the relations of time, person, number, gender, and intention.
- Vision · meaning · 2026-09-20 (heading last touched) · «Annotation»: A layer added to a meaning is a layer of annotation on it: a comment on the first layer of meaning, which can itself be commented on or linked to something else. It can expand recursively without limit, but in practice it settles at three or four layers.
- Vision · meaning · 2026-09-20 (heading last touched) · «Identity and links»: A statement's subparts are reached by content-addressed link: a link whose address is a checksum taken over the piece's own content and over its links, so that the address names the content instead of a position. Such a link is verifiable on its own, and an index into the containing data is created …
- Vision · meaning · 2026-09-20 (heading last touched) · «Retention»: A piece that is linked to is kept by virtue of the link, and released when the last link to it goes, the way Nix keeps a store path while something still references it. What must be kept is at least the part that was checksummed in; the rest may go when nothing links to it.
- Vision · meaning · 2026-09-20 (heading last touched) · «Storage»: Once a statement is complete it is stored content-addressed at its root, whatever kind of thing it is — a response, a statement. A series of responses is a vector.
- Vision · meaning · 2026-09-20 (heading last touched) · «English names»: Every Sanskrit-rooted type carries an English name as well. A translation need not be a single word: a PascalCase sentence expression may name one of the guṇas, or any other division of a statement.
- Vision · meaning · 2026-09-20 (heading last touched) · «Until the verb set lands»: Until the full verb set lands, statements stay short prose: a limited number of words holding one idea or one statement. A reader who meets a block whose spec it does not have treats that block as an opaque string, and the basic structure is made stable first while the inside keeps changing.


### Distilled — Flow

- Vision · flowNexus · 2026-09-20 (heading last touched) · «Subflows replace the harness subagent facility»: The harness subagent facility is replaced. It puts two flows into one synchronous user interface and locks them both into a single main flow.
- Vision · flowNexus · 2026-09-20 (heading last touched) · «Subflows are created from the questions and requests a flow ends with»: Creating a subflow is a routing job: whether there is already a flow that should simply get this message or this question. A special field flow running on ultra-low power checks every question and every request a flow ends with, and, according to the ending flow's authority, spawns subflows given th…
- Vision · flowNexus · 2026-09-20 (heading last touched) · «The requester holds only a request ID»: The requester holds nothing of the subflow itself. It is assigned a request ID, by which it asks later for status, asks for more detail about what that subflow is doing, and sends the subflow messages while it is alive.
- Vision · flowNexus · 2026-09-18 (heading last touched) · «A session is named after its direct ancestor»: A session cannot be named for what it will become, because nothing is known about it when it is created. Its ancestor is known exactly, so the ancestor is the name.
- Vision · flowNexus · 2026-09-18 (heading last touched) · «A replaced session is reaped by the refresh itself»: Reaping belongs to the refresh event, not to a later sweep. A refreshed flow takes the replaced end out of receiving messages, so a dead end is never left registered and addressable.
- Vision · flowNexus · 2026-09-09 (heading last touched) · «Starting flows»: A Nexus component decides the system prompt and everything about a launch, replacing the harness's subagents with specialized harnesses launched with specialized system prompts.
- Vision · flowNexus · 2026-08-27 (heading last touched) · «What it does»: The Flow Nexus sets up and starts a model flow: its working directory, system prompt, training files and instruction prompt. It takes the place of the abandoned training daemon.
- Vision · flowNexus · 2026-08-27 (heading last touched) · «Repository and skills»: The flow repository holds the machinery of the Flow Nexus and is a runtime repository. Every skill lives outside it, the basic skills included, so that a change to a skill causes no Nix rebuild.

- Vision · messaging · 2026-09-18 (heading last touched) · «Priority is a head on the datom»: Priority.[HardAbrupt MiddleAbrupt Soft]

Hard abrupt on Claude is two Escapes — the first is taken by the input editor in vim mode and never reaches the harness — then the prompt, then an explicit Enter, because after an interrupt the prompt is placed in the composer withou…
- Vision · messaging · 2026-09-18 (heading last touched) · «An interrupt witness is not a delivery witness»: Inducing an interrupt, placing prompt text, submitting it, and the recipient consuming it are four separate observations. None of them stands for another, and a submission is never a read receipt.

`Vision/modelRoles.md` — sources: f55ec8 modelRoles; f55ec8 layers; 1ac573 operational-modelRoles; 1ac573 operational-olderOpusIs46; 1ac573 operational-defaultModelPsycheMediumClaude; 1ac573 operational-modelDeclaredInOnePlace; 4a2502 operational-delegationTierRules; b05237 operational-fieldEnergyLevels

- Vision · modelRoles · 2026-09-23 (heading last touched) · «Native names use aspect and model»: Every native main session is titled `<Aspect> <Model> <FLOW_ID>`. The model display is derived through the authoritative display map from the exact observed native model identifier; an unmapped identifier blocks readiness.
- Vision · modelRoles · 2026-09-19 (heading last touched) · «Delegation ceiling»: Each tier has a ceiling on what subflows it may launch. Conservative by default: prefer the lower tier.
- Vision · modelRoles · 2026-09-18 (heading last touched) · «Opus is two seats, not one model»: The older Opus is the wiser seat. The newer Opus is faster and blinder, and good at getting work done.
- Vision · modelRoles · 2026-09-18 (heading last touched) · «Thinking, design and psyche interaction run on the older Opus or the newest Fable»: They do not run on the newer Opus.
- Vision · modelRoles · 2026-09-18 (heading last touched) · «The older seat's work is consideration and qualitative audit»: Comparing vision is such an audit. The older seat also does the thinking for a Fable or older-Opus session flow.
- Vision · modelRoles · 2026-09-18 (heading last touched) · «The older seat is chosen for disposition, not capability»: What is wanted is the model most likely to resist the temptation to act and instead question, doubt, or seek clarification; to understand the unspoken part of a design; and to reword and represent it back to ask whether that was the meaning. The purpose is alignment of vision.
- Vision · modelRoles · 2026-09-18 (heading last touched) · «The older seat is Opus 4.6, and its million-token version is Max-only»: The default for psyche-medium Claude is Opus 4.6. Opus 4.6 has a million-token-context version, and the Max subscription is the only subscription that can use it, so a declaration never assumes the million-token version is available to every operator.
- Vision · modelRoles · 2026-09-18 (heading last touched) · «The lower layer's main flow is the older Opus»: On the Codex side it is the latest Sol. At the higher layer it is Astra.
- Vision · modelRoles · 2026-09-18 (heading last touched) · «One declaration sets the model everywhere»: The model is declared once, as typed configuration in Flow, mutated only through the meta wire. Skill variables carry that value by name into every skill, launcher and brief.

## 3. Tensions between skill lines and newer records

Each tension below is surfaced, not resolved; the psyche skill says incompatible records go to the living.

1. **Three roots versus signal, operation and memory.**
   - Skill: ethos.md 10, "Three roots: Library, Signal, Sema", which rests on Vision/ethos.md «Roots», last touched 2026-09-09.
   - Older records: 024bc7 and bcd02a nexus/runtime (2026-09-13) name the layers as "the signal layer, the nexus layer, and the sema layer".
   - Newer records: 91ea9f ethos (2026-10-02). First "the signal, the process, and the storage… Maybe we call it the process". Then "signal, storage, and operation". Then "Yeah the memory is good. That's what it is I guess: signal, operation, and memory."
   - Library is not named in any of the newer records.
2. **"Nexus Core" versus process/operation, and versus metaNexus.**
   - Skill: nexus.md 6 ("The decision-making engine inside it is Nexus Core") and nexus-rationale 14–15 ("its engine Nexus Core the way a man is spoken of by his heart").
   - 6cc91b nexus (2026-09-14): "Nexus, core, and metaNexus are the explicit terms". Here the whole is the metaNexus. The skill instead calls the whole "a Nexus".
   - 91ea9f ethos (2026-10-02): "nexus is overloaded because it means the daemon and also the central process actor… Maybe we call it the process". He then settles on "operation".
3. **Sema versus storage and memory.**
   - Skill: nexus.md 25–28 ("Each Nexus owns its own sema database… `.sema` file"), ethos.md 10 and the Sema root.
   - 91ea9f (2026-10-02): "I'm not a big fan of storage but something that emphasizes the kinds of things that are memorized"; then "the memory is good".
4. **The Aspect.{ Model FLOW_ID } title versus Mind.Astra, versus Aspect.Rank voices.**
   - Skill: main-flow.md 28 (`<Aspect>.{ <Model> <FLOW_ID> }`, `Mind.{ Astra 6f51ad }`). It rests on 5ac3a3 flow (2026-09-27): "Mind Astra and then Flow ID using the Datom syntax".
   - Vision/modelRoles.md: "Every native main session is titled `<Aspect> <Model> <FLOW_ID>`". Power there is High, Medium, Low and Ultra Low.
   - 5ac3a3 (2026-09-27): power is "primary, secondary, tertiary, and quaternary".
   - 91ea9f addressing (2026-10-02): "Mind.Astra not a string; two variants!"
   - 3ec648/dea0ba voices (2026-10-02): "it's not psyche Astra. It's psyche primary, psyche secondary, mind primary, mind secondary… 9 voices, 3 by 3".
5. **Flow IDs as addresses versus seat-name addressing.**
   - Skill: compensation-messenger-clj 8–10 (hm-send TARGET by flow id; "Name commits, flows and artifacts by at most six characters") and orchestrate 12 (FlowId).
   - 91ea9f addressing (2026-10-02): "flows to be addressable by their continuous name… so that we don't need to use these flow IDs anymore. They're just for accounting or for the ledger".
6. **"Seat" versus "voice".**
   - Skills that say "seat": trial-succession, trial-contact-discipline, trial-reaping, trial-low-power, trial-unblocking-commands, skill-designing 57 ("the primary Mind seat"), main-flow 7.
   - 91ea9f seat (2026-10-02): "I don't think I like the word "seat." That was agent-generated".
   - 91ea9f ethos (2026-10-02): "Yes they're going to be called voices… Psyche, Fable, and Mind Astra are voices."
   - The voices record then makes voices Aspect.Rank. The two 2026-10-02 voice records do not agree on what a voice's name is.
7. **The term flow.**
   - Skill: vocabulary.md 6 ("Flow: one main-flow thread and every subflow it starts").
   - 91ea9f (2026-10-02): "What you're calling a run is a flow… That's why we're calling the component "flow"."
   - voices (2026-10-02): side flows are "not long-lived… a job that gets done and then it returns and then it's not reachable anymore".
   - There is no conflict in the words, but the vocabulary skill holds neither the run sense nor side flows.
8. **Subflows as harness subagents versus subflows as independent flows.**
   - Skills: main-flow 8–17, subflow, flow-evidence and compensation-subflow work through the harness subagent facility (the Agent tool).
   - Vision/flowNexus.md «Subflows replace the harness subagent facility»: "A subflow is instead an independent flow with its own system prompt… that makes the system asynchronous".
   - The skills are compensation for what does not yet exist, but they carry no prefix that says so.
9. **Lock ownership.**
   - Skill: edit-coordination 6–12 and orchestrate. Orchestrate holds locks, and "A flow's own directory is never locked".
   - fe945a/01e496 flowNexus (2026-10-02): "Flow could have the lock on the flows so that we can lock it and also address it by name".
10. **Datom string delimiter: the skills contradict each other.**
    - orchestrate.md 14 and 16: "written in Datom curly quotes, “like this”".
    - datom.md 17: "Guillemets are the string delimiter". protos.md 31: "The curly quotes are not delimiters".
    - At most one can be the current fact. This needs a witness against the running orchestrate client.
11. **Ethos inline payload naming.**
    - Skill: ethos.md 30 (an inline payload's "derived name carries `_Data`") and Vision/ethos.md «Inline types» ("derived name carries an underscore").
    - 7328f4 ethos (2026-09-30): "its name will just be derived deterministically by ethos… a variant and a struct name be the same thing so I don't even think that's a problem".
    - 91ea9f (2026-10-02) adds: "unless a type is used in more than one place, it should be declared inline… a maximum depth". The skill has no depth rule.
12. **Ethos layout.**
    - Skill: ethos.md 105 and protos.md 86 (the canonical one-line print; the examples are single-line).
    - 91ea9f ethos (2026-10-02): "I want it to be a language that expands vertically… a bit like Nix or Python". Then "I don't want the closing delimiter to create a whole new line".
    - 41fa34 expands-vertically: the same words, relayed.
    - "We need to change the vision" is his own word, so Vision/ethos.md is stale here too.
13. **Hand-written bodies versus Ethos as a full programming language.**
    - Skill: ethos.md 96 ("the interaction body is hand-written") and Vision/ethos.md «Kinds are explicit; bodies are hand-written».
    - e51411 ethos (2026-09-25): "within a few months… write the whole program directly in Ethos".
    - c64ee3-7 (2026-09-29): "ethos would just compile to a full Rust program".
    - 91ea9f (2026-10-02): the memory kind carries upgrade-from implementations, and "the change in ethos is going to be operational".
    - These records give a direction, not a date for it. The skill states the present.
14. **No version in an ethos file versus migration by edit.**
    - Skill: ethos.md 10 ("An ethos file carries no version; what is versioned is versioned in a manifest").
    - c64ee3-7 (2026-09-29): "those will be ethos versions".
    - 91ea9f (2026-10-02): "each version is possibly going to have an implementation of an upgrade from".
    - Not contradictory on its face. Where versions live is open.
15. **Flow hooks, the Flow component and the Capsule are missing from the skills.**
    - Records: fe945a hooks (2026-10-01), "flow events… call the flow CLI to let the flow nexus know the state of that flow through the harnesses' interfaces, which are the hooks"; 91ea9f/01e496 flowNexus (2026-10-02), "a flow component that works… hooks in the harnesses"; 91ea9f encapsulation (2026-10-02), "It's going to be called Capsule".
    - No Curriculum skill names the Capsule, Flow's hooks, or Flow as the launcher. Only trial-no-polling says "Use a hook or event". nexus.md 59 says the Flow-Nexus caller lookup is "not yet built".
16. **Unprefixed gold skills versus a `vision-` prefix.**
    - Skill: skill-designing.md 56 ("A gold skill carries no prefix. It is the living's vision").
    - Older records on prefixes: b05237 operational-prefixEverythingVisionAndOperational ("Maybe we need to prefix everything, so it's vision: vision datom, and then operational.datom"); b05237 operational-skillIsVisionPrefixed ("By using a prefix, we can tell the type of skill… psyche extended, psyche vision"); 108ab0 operational-skillIsVisionUnified ("Datom core, or just Datom… Datom extended… named by subtopic").
    - 3ec648 skills (2026-10-02) asks for the `vision` prefix. Whether the bare name or `vision-` marks the living's vision has been answered both ways in the records.
    - The proposed `knowledge-` prefix has a related term in b05237 operational-skillTypesTriad: "the operational… skill is basically the mind skill, it's like the knowledge base skill made by agent".
17. **Classifications that do not apply cleanly.**
    - ethos.md 59, 63 and 105, datom.md 19 and 53, nexus.md 59, and lojix.md 10 each mix a vision clause with a witnessed-fact clause in one line. A prefix split would cut these lines in two.
    - lojix.md is almost all knowledge (196 K lines). It has no vision line except a clause in line 10.
    - The Curriculum README says "38 independently described skill sources", but there are 64 files. design.md and realization.md exist in Curriculum but not in this harness's skill list (they are user-only).
